    Important Words
    • su - parṇena — by him of the great wings (Garuḍa)
      Compound: su (good, great) + parṇa (wing/feather). Suparṇa is one of Garuḍa's classical names; also denotes the soul in the Mundaka Upaniṣad image of two birds — though in Vaiṣṇava reading the two birds are jīva and Paramātmā.
    • tuṇḍa - pakṣa - nakheḥ — beak, wings, talons
      The three instruments by which Garuḍa fights — every part of the Lord's vāhana is weaponized. Gauḍīyas note that the Lord's associates need no separate armaments; their bodies are sufficient sevā-instruments.
    • kuru - udvaha — O hero of the Kurus (Parīkṣit)
      Compound: Kuru + udvaha (uplifter). Śuka turns to address his royal listener mid-narrative — a Bhāgavata convention that re-anchors the kathā in the speaker-hearer relationship that is itself bhakti.
    • narakaḥ — Naraka (Bhauma)
      Now alone on the field. The verse's syntax centers him for the climactic single combat to come — the Lord has cleared the periphery.
    Lord Hari then struck down all the missiles and weapons the enemy soldiers threw at Him, O hero of the Kurus, destroying each and every one with three sharp arrows. Meanwhile Garuḍa, as he carried the Lord, struck the enemy’s elephants with his wings. Beaten by Garuḍa’s wings, beak and talons, the elephants fled back into the city, leaving Narakāsura alone on the battlefield to oppose Kṛṣṇa.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Lord Hari then struck down all the missiles and weapons the enemy soldiers threw at Him, O hero of the Kurus, destroying each and every one with three sharp arrows. Meanwhile Garuḍa, as he carried the Lord, struck the enemy's elephants with his wings. Beaten by Garuḍa's wings, beak and talons, the elephants fled back into the city, leaving Narakāsura alone on the battlefield to oppose Kṛṣṇa.
    Commentary

    After describing cutting up the arms of the enemy troops, cutting up of their weapons is described. Astras are arrows and other missiles and sastras are swords and other hand weapons. One by one he shot down each weapon with three arrows. Before the weapons thrown could reach their target, he had killed the throwers of the weapons, and then destroyed the missiles while they were still in the air, each with three arrows. And the arrows were shot one after the other, not three at once. So quick was Krsna to shoot that in amazement Sukadeva cried out "O kurudvaha, Parksit!" Such swiftness had not be conceived by any of the Kurus such as Bhims and Arjuna though they had been taught by Krsna.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Śastra means swords etc. and astrāṇi means arrows etc. The Lord was carried on Garuḍa, who flew here and there with great speed at ease. This appeared most glorious and indicates Garuḍa’s power. Though he was carrying the Lord, with his wings he beat the elephants. From this it is understood that the fighters were not riding the elephants. The elephants were situated separately. Some were killed and some injured. Naraka, remaining in the battle (yudhi), continued to fight. This indicates that he was fearless.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Śastra means swords etc. and astrāṇi means arrows etc. The Lord was carried on Garuḍa, who flew here and there with great speed at ease. This appeared most glorious and indicates Garuḍa’s power. Though he was carrying the Lord, with his wings he beat the elephants. From this it is understood that the fighters were not riding the elephants. The elephants were situated separately. Some were killed and some injured. He attacked those in front with his beak, those on the sides with his wings, and those behind with his claws. This shows his speed and prowess. Naraka, remaining in the battle (yudhi), continued to fight. This indicates that he was fearless since he stayed after the elephants and troops were all defeated.

    • Padaseva Pr SB 10-59-17 Oriya
      His Grace Padaseva Prabhu· Oriya
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Garuḍa flogs the war-elephants with wings, beak, and talons until they retreat into the city — and Bhauma is left alone, exposed, like a man whose furniture has been removed mid-conversation. The Lord's strategy is not to overwhelm but to isolate; only when the soul is alone does it actually look up.

    Stories
    Garuḍa's pact

    Garuḍa is the eternal vāhana of Viṣṇu — Mahābhārata records his negotiation with Indra for the soma, and his eventual surrender to Viṣṇu, who placed him on His own flagstaff. From that day onward Garuḍa's labor is no longer for his mother's freedom from Kadrū's snakes; it is for the Lord's līlā.

    In this chapter, Garuḍa beats elephants with wings and talons exactly as he would treat the snakes he natively eats. The Vaiṣṇava reading: every faculty turned toward the Lord regains its proper appetite.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Important Words
    • śaktyā — with his spear
      Instrumental of śakti — the same word that names spiritual potency in Gauḍīya doctrine. Bhauma's weapon is a 'śakti' in the war-sense; the Lord's are the eternal hlādinī and saṁvit śaktis. Same word, opposite registers.
    • vajraḥ — Indra's thunderbolt
      Indra's premier weapon, said to be forged from the sage Dadhīci's bones. The spear that bested vajra is no trifle — the verse establishes Bhauma's credentials precisely to make their irrelevance to Garuḍa more striking.
    • mālā - āhataḥ — struck by a garland
      The chapter's most concentrated image: a war-spear treated as a garland. Gauḍīyas read it as a glimpse of Vaikuṇṭha-mood — where weapons of māyā arrive as flowers in the Lord's domain.
    • dvipaḥ — elephant
      Lit. 'two-drinker' (drinks with trunk and mouth). Elephants are unmoved by flower-strikes; the simile lets Garuḍa's stature borrow the elephant's bulk while the spear borrows the flower's lightness.
    Seeing his army driven back and tormented by Garuḍa, Bhauma attacked him with his spear, which had once defeated Lord Indra’s thunderbolt. But though struck by that mighty weapon, Garuḍa was not shaken. Indeed, he was like an elephant hit with a flower garland.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Seeing his army driven back and tormented by Garuḍa, Bhauma attacked him with his spear, which had once defeated Lord Indra's thunderbolt. But though struck by that mighty weapon, Garuḍa was not shaken. Indeed, he was like an elephant hit with a flower garland.
    Commentary

    Seeing Bhauma with the lance in his hand, Satyabhama said, "Kill him quickly." Therfore Krsna killed him then.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    He saw his troops scattered (vidrāvitam) since they were afflicted by Garuḍa. Or he saw the troops somewhat scattered and somewhat afflicted. The army suffered because the elephants were their mainstay (and had fled). Or sainya can include the elephant drivers and horsemen. Struck by the weapon, Garuḍa did not feel pain (he did not shake). A condition is indicated by its external symptoms. Or, Garuḍa did not move (did not shake) from his place and his determination.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    A spear that once defeated Indra's vajra strikes Garuḍa, and he does not even flinch — like an elephant pelted with a garland. The verse rewrites the cosmic power-hierarchy in one image: what cracks heaven's king is, against the Lord's bird, an offering of flowers.

    Important Words
    • śūlam — trident
      The same word as in verse 7 (Mura's trident) — the chapter's recurring weapon. By the third time it appears, the reader knows it never lands; the Bhāgavatam teaches by repetition that asura-weapons against Bhagavān are decorative.
    • vitatha - udyamaḥ — whose endeavors were frustrated
      Compound: vitatha (false, in vain) + udyama (effort). Vitatha is a precise Sanskrit term — 'made false', shown to be groundless. All of Bhauma's vikrama is finally a vitatha.
    • cakreṇa — with His disc
      Instrumental of cakra — the Sudarśana, 'auspicious vision'. Reserved for the killing blow against Bhauma, as against Mura. The disc moves at the speed of the Lord's intention.
    • kṣura - neminā — whose edge is razor-sharp
      Compound: kṣura (razor) + nemi (rim, edge). The Sudarśana's circumference is its blade — a precise descriptive detail Gauḍīya kathākāras savor when describing the Lord's weapons.
    Bhauma, frustrated in all his attempts, took up his trident to kill Lord Kṛṣṇa. But even before he could release it, the Lord cut off his head with His razor-sharp cakra as the demon sat atop his elephant.
    Purport

    According to Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī, as Bhauma raised his invincible trident, Satyabhāmā, sitting on Garuḍa with the Lord, said to Kṛṣṇa, “Kill him right away,” and Kṛṣṇa did just that.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Bhauma, frustrated in all his attempts, took up his trident to kill Lord Kṛṣṇa. But even before he could release it, the Lord cut off his head with His razor-sharp cakra as the demon sat atop his elephant.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    He cut off his head even before Bhauma could release his spear. He took off the head as if Bhauma was unaware (apa—aharat) because he used the cakra with sharp blades on its spokes.

    Before the executioner can even raise the axe, the king's word has already removed the head. Bhauma reaches for the trident; the cakra has already arrived. Gauḍīyas note that Bhagavān's response is faster than the asura's intention — grace is always pre-existent.

    Important Words
    • kuṇḍalam — earrings
      Bhauma's helmet bears the same word that will appear in verse 23 for Aditi's stolen earrings — the chapter circulates this jewelry: stolen, worn in pride, fallen with the head, returned to Aditi. Material objects also follow karmic gravity.
    • samujjvalam — brilliant
      From sam + ud + √jval (to blaze). Even the asura's severed head 'blazes' — Bhāgavata aesthetics never strips even an enemy of his beauty at the moment of falling under Bhagavān's hand.
    • mukundam — Lord Kṛṣṇa, the giver of liberation
      From mukti (liberation) + da (giver). One of Kṛṣṇa's most beloved names — chosen here at the precise moment when an asura is sent home. The Lord who is now showered with flowers has just given mukti to a son of the earth.
    • ṛṣayaḥ — the sages
      The Bhāgavatam's standing chorus — sages above always recognize what humans below miss. Their 'sādhu, sādhu' here is the audible verdict of the universe's wisest beings on what just happened.
    Fallen on the ground, Bhaumāsura’s head shone brilliantly, decorated as it was with earrings and an attractive helmet. As cries of “Alas, alas!” and “Well done!” arose, the sages and principal demigods worshiped Lord Mukunda by showering Him with flower garlands.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Fallen on the ground, Bhaumāsura's head shone brilliantly, decorated as it was with earrings and an attractive helmet. As cries of "Alas, alas!" and "Well done!" arose, the sages and principal demigods worshiped Lord Mukunda by showering Him with flower garlands.
    Commentary

    The rsis and devatas shouted "Lamentable! Very good!" "Ha" usually indicates sorrow but here indicates condemnation according to Ksira swami. "Oh what a sinful person , disturbing the whole world! It is good that you have killed him." They covered Krsna with garlands.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The head was attractive to people because it was the head of a demon that had fallen on the ground. Though the Lord created beauty, now it was confirmed. Thus in joy the head is described as shining (samujjvalam). The earrings were somewhat a cause of the head’s shining. Some who were very sinful at that place shouted in condemnation “Hā, hā!” This is according to Kṣīrasvāmī. The sages said (verb should be added) in joy “Good!” The devatās like Indra, covering Kṛṣṇa with garlands, worshipped him.

    A head bejewelled and decapitated lies on the ground gleaming like a fallen lamp — even in death the asura's ornaments shine because they came from the demigods he plundered. Sages above call out 'sādhu, sādhu'; flower garlands drop from the sky. Death-by-Bhagavān is the only end the sages will applaud.

    Maṇi-parvata, the peak of Mandara Mountain .
    Important Words
    • bhūḥ — the goddess of the earth
      Bhū-devī, Mother Earth — Bhauma's birth-mother. Gauḍīyas note that she appears precisely after her son's death; the same Earth that grieved Parīkṣit's curse in SB 1.16 grieves here too, and her grief is the doorway to her praise.
    • kuṇḍale — the two earrings
      Dual — Aditi's pair of earrings, originally given by Lord Viṣṇu (per tradition) and stolen by Bhauma. The pair circles the chapter; their return motivates Kṛṣṇa's later journey to Indra's abode (verse 38).
    • vaijayantyā — the Vaijayantī garland
      Vaijayantī = 'victory-flag'. A garland of five-colored forest flowers, traditionally worn by Viṣṇu (Bhāgavatam 8.18.2 describes Vāmana wearing it). Bhūmi restores to Kṛṣṇa what is iconographically already His.
    • mahā - maṇim — the great jewel (Mandara's peak)
      Compound: mahā (great) + maṇi (jewel). The Maṇi-parvata — Mandara's peak — was the demigods' recreation ground (the site of the churning of the milk-ocean in earlier cantos). Bhauma had taken it; Bhūmi returns it.
    The goddess of the earth then approached Lord Kṛṣṇa and presented Him with Aditi’s earrings, which were made of glowing gold inlaid with shining jewels. She also gave Him a Vaijayantī flower garland, Varuṇa’s umbrella and the peak of Mandara Mountain.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    The goddess of the earth then approached Lord Kṛṣṇa and presented Him with Aditi's earrings, which were made of glowing gold inlaid with shining jewels. She also gave Him a Vaijayantī flower garland, Varuṇa's umbrella and the peak of Mandara Mountain.
    Commentary

    Bhumi returned everything stolen by her son, including the jewel mountain (maha manim).

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The Vaijayantī is a five colored garland and vanamālā garland made of leaves and flowers, extending to the feet. She offered him the earrings along with a vanamāla and vaijyayanti garland.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Bhū lived always in the house of Naraka, being his mother. Or she appeared at that time. She approached Kṛṣṇa, directly bhagavān, and her husband. The Vaijayantī is a five colored garland and the vanamālā garland made of leaves and flowers, extending to the feet. She offered him the earrings along with a vanamāla and vaijyayanti garland. Atha indicates all or after some time. After that she offered the umbrella and mountain.

    Analogy

    A mother holds the stolen goods of her wayward son in trembling hands and offers them, with him gone, to the Lord who took him — Bhūmi's approach to Kṛṣṇa is the gesture of a grieving parent returning what was never truly hers. The earrings, the garland, the umbrella, the mountain-peak: a heap of borrowed cosmos restored to its source.

    Important Words
    • astauṣīt — she praised
      Imperfect of √stu (to praise). Stava/stotra is one of the nine limbs of bhakti (śravaṇam kīrtanam viṣṇoḥ...). Bhūmi's prayer that follows is a model stava — Gauḍīyas use it as one of several earth-goddess hymns alongside the prayers of Pṛthu's mother and other devotee-mothers.
    • prāñjaliḥ — with folded palms
      Compound: pra (forward) + añjali (cupped palms). The standard posture of supplication — Gauḍīyas note that bhakti always finds its hands first.
    • bhakti - pravaṇayā — with a mentality inclined toward devotion
      Compound: bhakti + pravaṇa (inclined, sloped toward). Bhakti is here described as the heart's natural slope — water finds the low ground; the devoted heart finds Bhagavān.
    • viśva - īśam — Lord of the universe
      Compound: viśva (universe) + īśa (Lord). The first epithet Bhūmi reaches for is cosmic — her grief is contained, theology comes first.
    O King, after bowing down to Him and then standing with joined palms, the goddess, her mind filled with devotion, began to praise the Lord of the universe, whom the best of demigods worship.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    O King, after bowing down to Him and then standing with joined palms, the goddess, her mind filled with devotion, began to praise the Lord of the universe, whom the best of demigods worship.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    After offering the stolen items, she, overwhelmed with devotion in her mind (dhiyā), in order to praise him, first offered respects and then folded her hands. Or lowering her head and folding her hands she began praising him, since he was Lord of the universe and thus worshipped by the best devatās like Brahmā. She praised him with such words.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    After offering the stolen items, she, overwhelmed with devotion in her mind (dhiyā), in order to praise him, first offered respects and then folded her hands. Or lowering her head and folding her hands, she began praising him, since he was Lord of the universe and thus worshipped by the best devatās like Brahmā. She praised him with such words. This was because she was dear to the Lord (devī) or shining (devī) with bhakti. This indicates that she knew the glories of the Lord. O king, please understand the meaning of the prayers.

    Important Words
    • bhūmiḥ uvāca — the earth-goddess said
      Bhū-devī's prayer begins here. Gauḍīyas treat earth-goddess stavas with special tenderness — Bhūmi is herself a consort of Viṣṇu (as Bhū-śakti); her prayer is from inside His circle, not outside.
    • deva - deva — Lord of lords
      Reduplicated devatā address — devānāṁ devaḥ, the deva above devas. The standard top-of-hierarchy invocation; placed first in the prayer.
    • bhakta - icchā - upātta - rūpāya — to You who assume forms according to Your devotees' desire
      Compound chain: bhakta (devotee) + icchā (desire) + upātta (taken up) + rūpa (form). Foundational Vaiṣṇava theology — Bhagavān's various avatāras and rūpas are not necessitated by His nature but elected for His devotees. Gauḍīya commentaries make this the verse's hinge.
    • parama - ātman — O Supreme Soul
      Compound: parama (highest) + ātman (self). The localized Lord in the heart — Paramātmā is the Gauḍīya category that names the Lord's pervasive in-dwelling, distinct from the jīva-ātmā and from Bhagavān's full svayaṁ-rūpa.
    Goddess Bhūmi said: Obeisances unto You, O Lord of the chief demigods, O holder of the conchshell, disc and club. O Supreme Soul within the heart, You assume Your various forms to fulfill Your devotees’ desires. Obeisances unto You.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Goddess Bhūmi said: Obeisances unto You, O Lord of the chief demigods, O holder of the conchshell, disc and club. O Supreme Soul within the heart, You assume Your various forms to fulfill Your devotees' desires. Obeisances unto You.
    Commentary

    "You know my sincere heart, since you are paramatma, even though I am the mother of someone opposed to you."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    You are the protector of devatās like Brahmā (deva-deveśa). Therefore you hold the conch to kill the wicked. You appear in various forms like Matsya by the desire of your devotees. You give them the proper intelligence (paramātman).

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    You are the protector of devatās like Brahmā (deva-deveśa). Therefore you hold the conch, disc and club to kill the wicked. You appear in various forms like Matsya by the desire of your devotees. You give them the proper intelligence (paramātman). The order of holding the weapons is from lower left hand, and ending with the right lower. Since his consort is situated on his left, she is mentioned first from the left. At this time he was seen for four arms, as Nārada sees him. “Or deva-deveśa means Nārāyaṇa.” That is true. He is you. Thus the four armed form is described holding the conch etc. “Why, if I am Nārāyaṇa, am I also two armed?” You assume forms according to the desire of your devotee. Appearing as a human you are beautiful. By your power you spread everywhere or you are the dearest to all people. Paramātman!

She speaks to Him as Paramātmā first, the One who takes forms according to devotees' desire; only later (verse 31) will she ask for her grandson. Bhakti orders its own grammar: praise first, plea last.

    unto You, the soles of whose feet are engraved with lotus flowers (and who are therefore said to possess lotus feet) .
    Important Words
    • paṅkaja - nābhāya — to the lotus-naveled Lord
      Compound: paṅkaja (lotus, 'mud-born') + nābha (navel). From Viṣṇu's navel rises the lotus on which Brahmā sits — the entire cosmos sprouts from this one organ. Gauḍīyas keep this image in front of any reading of cosmology.
    • paṅkaja - māline — to the wearer of a lotus garland
      Compound: paṅkaja + mālin (garlanded). The Lord's vana-mālā — the forest-flower garland — frequently includes lotuses; an aesthetic detail Bhūmi savors.
    • paṅkaja - netrāya — to the lotus-eyed Lord
      Compound: paṅkaja + netra (eye). Padma-locana is one of Kṛṣṇa's most cherished epithets — His glance, cool and elongated like a lotus petal. Gauḍīyas spend entire ślokas on this glance.
    • paṅkaja - aṅghraye — to the lotus-footed Lord
      Compound: paṅkaja + aṅghri (foot). Lotus feet — the soles marked with lotus, the destination of all śaraṇāgati. Caitanya Mahāprabhu's entire Śikṣāṣṭaka is, in effect, a meditation on these feet.
    My respectful obeisances are unto You, O Lord, whose abdomen is marked with a depression like a lotus flower, who are always decorated with garlands of lotus flowers, whose glance is as cool as the lotus and whose feet are engraved with lotuses.
    Purport

    Queen Kuntī offered this same prayer, which is found in First Canto of the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam , Chapter 8, text 22. The synonyms and translation given here are taken from Śrīla Prabhupāda’s rendering of that text. We may also note that although Kuntī’s prayer occurs early in the Bhāgavatam, she offered it many years after the incident described here.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    My respectful obeisances are unto You, O Lord, whose abdomen is marked with a depression like a lotus flower, who are always decorated with garlands of lotus flowers, whose glance is as cool as the lotus and whose feet are engraved with lotuses.
    Commentary

    She described the sweet beauty of Krsna, indicating that when Krsna came before her, her senses had reached their perfection.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    This verse elaborates on the phrase deva-deveśa of the last verse and praises Kṛṣṇa. Śrīdhara Svāmī in his commentary makes lotus feet in the dual form, since Kṛṣṇa has two feet, even though only one of his feet is marked with the lotus. Using the word “lotus” can also be interpreted as an expression of the Lord’s beauty. His navel is compared to a lotus because his navel is deep like a lake, from which the lotus of Brahmā sprouts, or because Kṛṣṇa has a navel shaped like a lotus. The first three elements—navel, garland and eyes--are described in order of increasing beauty and consequently excellence. The feet are placed last to indicate beauty as an expression of devotion.

    Analogy

    Four times Bhūmi says 'lotus' — His navel, His garland, His glance, His feet. The flower the rest of the world only borrows for offering, He is. Bhakti's poetic instinct: when language begins to repeat, it has found the right object.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.59.27

    ↗
    नमो भगवते तुभ्यं वासुदेवाय विष्णवे । पुरुषायादिबीजाय पूर्णबोधाय ते नम: ॥ २७ ॥
    namo bhagavate tubhyaṁ
    [namo bhagavate tubhyam]
    vāsudevāya viṣṇave
    [vaasudevaaya vishnave]
    puruṣāyādi-bījāya
    [purushaayaadi-beejaaya]

    [poorna-bodhaaya te namah]
    Word-for-word
    namaḥ — obeisances bhagavate — to the Supreme Godhead tubhyam — unto You vāsudevāya — Lord Vāsudeva, the shelter of all created beings viṣṇave — all-pervading Lord Viṣṇu puruṣāya — the primeval person ādi — original bījāya — the seed pūrṇa — full bodhāya — knowledge te — to You namaḥ — obeisances .
    Blended reading (Sanskrit + English inline)

    Important Words
    • vāsudevāya — to Lord Vāsudeva
      Vāsudeva — Kṛṣṇa as son of Vasudeva, and also (etymologically) 'the indwelling deva'. Gauḍīyas read Vāsudeva as the first of the catur-vyūha (Vāsudeva-Saṅkarṣaṇa-Pradyumna-Aniruddha) and as the form addressed in the dvādaśākṣarī mantra Om namo bhagavate vāsudevāya.
    • viṣṇave — to all-pervading Viṣṇu
      From √viś (to enter, pervade). Viṣṇu is Bhagavān in His pervasive aspect; Gauḍīyas hold that all Viṣṇu-forms are expansions of Kṛṣṇa, the svayaṁ-bhagavān (SB 1.3.28).
    • ādi - bījāya — to the original seed
      Compound: ādi (first) + bīja (seed). Bīja is also the technical term for the seed-syllable of a mantra. The Lord is both source of creation and the syllable from which mantra-power unfolds.
    • pūrṇa - bodhāya — to the fully-aware one
      Compound: pūrṇa (complete) + bodha (knowledge). Bhagavān is not 'omniscient' as an attribute — He is the wholeness of awareness itself. Gauḍīyas distinguish pūrṇa-bodha from impersonalist 'pure consciousness': the fullness here is personal.
    Obeisances unto You, the Supreme Lord Vāsudeva, Viṣṇu, the primeval person, the original seed. Obeisances unto You, the omniscient one.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Obeisances unto You, the Supreme Lord Vāsudeva, Viṣṇu, the primeval person, the original seed. Obeisances unto You, the omniscient one.
    Commentary

    I am playing in the ocean of your sweetness and aisvarya. I pay respects to you, full of all powers( bhagavate). But though are full of all powers, you are the son of Vasudeva (vasudevaya), but still you are the original form of bhagavan. Though you are the son of Vasudeva, you are also Visnu, the all pervading. Though you are all pervading, you are still a person (purusa), individualized. Though you are individualized, you are the source of all (adi bija), even of Visnu, as shown during the Brahma mohana lila. Though you are such a seed, you are form of complete consciousness itself, that is brahman (purna bodha). In other words, though you are endowed with infinite spiritual qualities, you are also the impersonal brahma. Though you are unborn (aja), you generate the universes. Though you generate the universes, you are impersonal brahman. Though you are impersonal and without qualities, you are endowed with form and unlimited qualities and energies (ananta saktaye). Though you have unlimited energies, you have three principal energies: tatashta, bahiranga and antaranga. You are the jiva composed of all the elevated and degraded souls (para avara atma), you are the body made of the five gross material elements (bhuta atma) and you are the internal witness, paramatma.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Such beauty is befitting. That is expressed in this verse. You are the form who eagerly reveals unlimited qualities (bhagavān) since you act as the son of Vasudeva. Thus you spread the universe with your glories and are the seed for your devotees (puruṣa) gaining (āya) prema. There is no need for the devotees to inform you about their desire since you are full of knowledge.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Such beauty is befitting. That is expressed in this verse. You are the form who eagerly reveals unlimited qualities (bhagavān) since you act as the son of Vasudeva. Thus you spread the universe with your glories. You are internal and external. You exist in the hearts of all as antaryāmī (puruṣāya). Moreover you are the source of sweet pastimes (ādi-bījāya). Otherwise you would not show your infant and other pastimes. Or you are the cause for your devotees (puruṣa) gaining (āya) prema. There is no need for the devotees to inform you about their desire, since you are full of knowledge (pūrṇa-bodhāya). Or you give understanding of bhakti to the ātmārāmas, making liberation of the ātmārāmas insignificant. Kunti says: tathā paramahaṁsānāṁ munīnām amalātmanām | bhakti-yoga-vidhānārthaṁ kathaṁ paśyema hi striyaḥ || How can we women hope to see you, the object of bhakti for the omniscient, liberated sages? SB 1.10.8

    Related verses

    Concepts
    AtmaYoga
    Related lectures
    • Padaseva Pr SB 10-59-27 Oriya
      His Grace Padaseva Prabhu· Oriya
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Bhūmi names Him three ways — Vāsudeva, Viṣṇu, Puruṣa — then ādi-bīja, original seed, then pūrṇa-bodha, complete knowledge. The list moves from form to source to substance; it is a small Bhāgavatam inside a single ślokā.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.59.28

    ↗
    अजाय जनयित्रेऽस्य ब्रह्मणेऽनन्तशक्तये । परावरात्मन् भूतात्मन् परमात्मन् नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ २८ ॥
    ajāya janayitre ’sya
    [ajaaya janayitre ’sya]
    brahmaṇe ’nanta-śaktaye
    [brahmane ’nanta-shaktaye]
    parāvarātman bhūtātman
    [paraavaraatman bhootaatman]

    Word-for-word
    ajāya — to the unborn janayitre — the progenitor asya — of this (universe) brahmaṇe — the Absolute ananta — unlimited śaktaye — whose energies para — of the superior avara — and the inferior ātman — O Soul bhūta — of the material creation ātman — O Soul parama - ātman — O Supreme Soul, who are all-pervading namaḥ — obeisances astu — may there be te — unto You .

    Important Words
    • ajāya — to the unborn
      Aja — 'unborn'. Standard Upaniṣadic epithet (Kaṭha, Śvetāśvatara). Gauḍīyas read it as denoting Bhagavān's transcendence of conditioned birth while still allowing His descents (avatāras) — which appear-as-birth but are not.
    • brahmaṇe — to the Absolute (Brahman)
      Dative of Brahman. In Gauḍīya theology, Brahman names the impersonal aspect — only the bodily effulgence of Bhagavān. The same Person Bhūmi addresses includes Brahman; He is its source.
    • ananta - śaktaye — to the One whose energies are unlimited
      Compound: ananta (endless) + śakti (potency). Bhagavān has innumerable śaktis, classically grouped into three (cit, jīva, māyā). 'Unlimited' is not a vague intensifier here — it is theological precision.
    • parama - ātman — O Supreme Soul
      Recurs from verse 25. Bhūmi's prayer cycles through Paramātmā multiple times — He is what she is grieving toward, the Soul of her dead son and of herself.
    Obeisances unto You of unlimited energies, the unborn progenitor of this universe, the Absolute. O Soul of the high and the low, O Soul of the created elements, O all-pervading Supreme Soul, obeisances unto You.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Obeisances unto You of unlimited energies, the unborn progenitor of this universe, the Absolute. O Soul of the high and the low, O Soul of the created elements, O all-pervading Supreme Soul, obeisances unto You.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Though you are the son of Vasudeva, you have all powers. That is explained in this verse.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    You are without a cause, existing at all times (ajāya). You are the soul of causes like mahat-tattva and effects like the universe. Or you are the soul of elevated beings like Brahmā and low beings like trees. You are the soul of the jīvas. Not only one soul exists but the many jīvas who are different. And you are their antaryāmī (paramātman). Or though you have a limited form as the son of Vasudeva, you are spread everywhere inside and outside, having unlimited, astonishing powers. That is described in the verse. You are not transformed with birth (ajāya) or the stages following birth. You are the cause of the universe. “But is it not contradictory to be without transformation and to create the universe?” Because you are the supreme Lord it is possible. You are the form of Brahman. You possess unlimited powers. Or the verse shows that the Lord is the source of all attainments of the devotees. I offer respects to the cause of understanding of Brahmā, or the cause of the universe for Lord Brahmā (brahmaṇe janayitre). Showing that the puruṣa is the source of Brahmā, that form is further described. I offer respects to that universe, which arises from Visnu (ajāya). It is caused by the Lord’s unlimited śakti. It is astonishing. By this creation, the Lord’s great affection for Brahmā is shown. Or “Does Brahmā create the universe?” Yes. But by your astonishing śakti (ananta-śaktaye). “For my devotees it is fine, but why promote bhakti to ātmārāmas?” It is because of the strength of your mercy. O soul of fathers (para) and mothers (avara)! Promoter of caring for sons with affection! You have greater mercy than parents. O dearest to all (paramātmān)! Everyone’s mercy arises because they attain a drop of your ocean of mercy.

      His Grace Padaseva Prabhu· Oriya
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Bhūmi addresses Him as ananta-śakti — unlimited in His energies — and then names Him 'Soul of the superior and the inferior'. The pair (para / avara) is not a hierarchy He surveys from outside; He is the Soul of both. Gauḍīya acintya-bhedābheda compressed: same in essence, distinct in expression.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.59.29

    ↗
    त्वं वै सिसृक्षुरज उत्कटं प्रभो तमो निरोधाय बिभर्ष्यसंवृत: । स्थानाय सत्त्वं जगतो जगत्पते काल: प्रधानं पुरुषो भवान् पर: ॥ २९ ॥
    tvaṁ vai sisṛkṣur aja utkaṭaṁ prabho
    [tvam vai sisrikshur aja utkatam prabho]
    tamo nirodhāya bibharṣy asaṁvṛtaḥ
    [tamo nirodhaaya bibharshy asamvritah]
    sthānāya sattvaṁ jagato jagat-pate
    [sthaanaaya sattvam jagato jagat-pate]
    kālaḥ pradhānaṁ puruṣo bhavān paraḥ
    [kaalah pradhaanam purusho bhavaan parah]
    Word-for-word
    tvam — You vai — indeed sisṛkṣuḥ — desiring to create ajaḥ — unborn utkaṭam — prominent prabho — O master tamaḥ — the mode of ignorance nirodhāya — for annihilation bibharṣi — You assume asaṁvṛtaḥ — uncovered sthānāya — for maintenance sattvam — the mode of goodness jagataḥ — of the universe jagat - pate — O Lord of the universe kālaḥ — time pradhānam — material nature (in its original, undifferentiated state) puruṣaḥ — the creator (who interacts with material nature) bhavān — You paraḥ — distinct .

    Important Words
    • sisṛkṣuḥ — desiring to create
      Desiderative of √sṛj (to create). The form itself encodes the verse's point — creation begins with the Lord's intention; it is not a mechanical emanation.
    • asaṁvṛtaḥ — uncovered (by the modes)
      A- (not) + sam + √vṛ (covered together). The crucial Gauḍīya word: even when Bhagavān 'assumes' a guṇa, He is not covered by it — He stands clear of His own instrumentation. Sets Vaiṣṇava theology against any pantheist conflation.
    • pradhānam — material nature (in its undifferentiated state)
      Pradhāna — Sāṅkhya's term for primal prakṛti. Bhūmi names the technical Sāṅkhya categories (kāla, pradhāna, puruṣa) and then declares Him distinct from all three — Vaiṣṇavism's classical move against any monist collapse.
    • jagat - pate — O Lord of the universe
      Compound: jagat (universe, lit. 'the moving') + pati (lord). Jagat literally means 'that which goes' — the universe as motion. Bhagavān is its master.
    • paraḥ — transcendent, separate
      From pṛ (to lead beyond). The verse's final word — Bhūmi insists on the Lord's distinct, transcendent identity even after listing every category He inhabits.
    Desiring to create, O unborn master, You increase and then assume the mode of passion. You do likewise with the mode of ignorance when You wish to annihilate the universe and with goodness when You wish to maintain it. Nonetheless, You remain uncovered by these modes. You are time, the pradhāna, and the puruṣa, O Lord of the universe, yet still You are separate and distinct.
    Purport

    The word jagataḥ in the third line of this verse indicates that the functions of creation, maintenance and annihilation are here mentioned in a cosmic context. The word utkaṭam indicates that when a particular function is being carried out, whether universal creation, maintenance or annihilation, the particular material quality associated with that function becomes predominant.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Desiring to create, O unborn master, You increase and then assume the mode of passion. You do likewise with the mode of ignorance when You wish to annihilate the universe and with goodness when You wish to maintain it. Nonetheless, You remain uncovered by these modes. You are time, the pradhāna, and the puruṣa, O Lord of the universe, yet still You are separate and distinct.
    Commentary

    According to the time, you carry out creation, maintenance and destruction of the universe through your maya sakti. You contain the activated raja, tama and sattva gunas, but are not covered by them like the jivas. Time, maya and the jivas are all eternal and are yourself, being your energies, but you are different from all these energies by your svarupa sakti (parah).

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    You alone (vai) accept rajoguṇa (utkaṭam) since creation cannot take place with equilibrium. O lord, you are capable of creating this variegated universe (prabho). By your nature you protect the universe (jagat-pate). You are not covered by the actions of creation, maintenance and destruction. Or though you accept ignorance for destruction, you are not covered by it.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Bhakti (Devotion)Samata (Equanimity / Even-mindedness)Vairagya (Detachment / Dispassion)Karuna (Kindness / Compassion)Svadhyaya (Self-study & Scripture Study)
    Related verses
    BG 14.1 BG 9.11 BG 12.6 BG 17.3 BG 5.18 BG 18.1 BG 3.10 BG 18.64 BG 10.3 BG 10.4 BG 7.8 BG 18.37 SB 4.29.1 BG 2.47
    Concepts
    Sattva
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    A king plays three roles in his own court — he commissions the play, performs the play, and watches the play — yet remains the king throughout. Bhagavān assumes rajas to create, tamas to dissolve, sattva to maintain; the modes are His costumes, never His skin. Gauḍīyas insist on this distinction against any reading that makes Him reducible to prakṛti's drift.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.59.30

    ↗
    अहं पयो ज्योतिरथानिलो नभो मात्राणि देवा मन इन्द्रियाणि । कर्ता महानित्यखिलं चराचरं त्वय्यद्वितीये भगवन्नयं भ्रम: ॥ ३० ॥
    ahaṁ payo jyotir athānilo nabho

    Important Words
    • advitīye — in You who have no second
      Advitīya — without a second. Famously appropriated by Advaitins; Gauḍīyas read it as Bhagavān's incomparability — no second 'parallel God', not 'no second consciousness'. Acintya-bhedābheda holds the jīva as eternally distinct yet never independent.
    • kartā — 'the doer' (false ego)
      From √kṛ (to do). Here used as a noun for ahaṅkāra — the I-the-doer sense. The Gītā's same point: the body acts under the modes, the ego claims doership, the soul is something else.
    • mahān — the mahat-tattva (total material energy)
      Mahat — the first material principle, from which ahaṅkāra and the rest unfold. Bhūmi's list is technical Sāṅkhya, but oriented theistically.
    • bhramaḥ — illusion
      From √bhram (to wander, err). 'Illusion' here is the misperception that the categories are independent of Bhagavān — not (as in Advaita) that the world is unreal. The Bhāgavatam's realism keeps the world real; only its independence is illusion.
    This is illusion: that earth, water, fire, air, ether, sense objects, demigods, mind, the senses, false ego and the total material energy exist independent of You. In fact, they are all within You, my Lord, who are one without a second.
    Purport

    The earth-goddess, in her prayers, directly touches upon the subtleties of transcendental philosophy, clarifying that although the Supreme Lord is unique and distinct from His creation, His creation has no independent existence and always rests within Him. Thus the Lord and His creation are simultaneously one and different, as explained by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu five hundred years ago. To say that everything is God, without any distinction, is meaningless, since nothing can act like God. Dogs, shoes and human beings are hardly omnipotent or omniscient, nor do they create the universe. On the other hand, there is a real sense in which all things are one, for everything is part of the same supreme, absolute reality. Lord Caitanya has given the very useful analogy of the sun and the sun rays. The sun and its sunshine are one reality, for the sun is the celestial body that shines. On the other hand, one can certainly distinguish between the sun globe and the sun rays. Thus God’s simultaneous oneness with and difference from His creation is the final and satisfying explanation of reality. All that exists is the Lord’s potency, and yet He endows the superior potency, the living beings, with free will so that they can become responsible for the moral and spiritual quality of their decisions and activities. This entire transcendental science is clearly and rationally explained in the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    This is illusion: that earth, water, fire, air, ether, sense objects, demigods, mind, the senses, false ego and the total material energy exist independent of You. In fact, they are all within You, my Lord, who are one without a second.
    Commentary

    " If you say that the material body, the senses, the gunas are me, brahman colored by maya, then how can you say that I am also separate?" "Those people who say that earth and all the other elements, the senses, the tan mantras (matrani), mind, ahankara (karta), and citta (mahan) the moving (mind and senses) and non moving (dull matter and prana) exist in you are mistaken, because in you there is no difference (advitiye). Your body and senses are all spiritual. It cannot be that matter is the same as you."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    “If the Lord accepts guṇas, how can he be supreme?” The word atha indicates a continuous list of the elements. The word ca (in another version) indicates buddhi and citta. They all exist in you. You are without a second since all this is your śakti and you are the śaktimān and nothing exists except you since the items are only the effect. All of what was mentioned, moving and non-moving beings, the effects, are understood to be illusory. Since you are embraced by your antaraṅga-śakti, you are not touched by effects of the bahiraṅga-śakti. The effect of ether is air for instance, but ether remains untouched. tvam ādyaḥ puruṣaḥ sākṣād īśvaraḥ prakṛteḥ paraḥ māyāṁ vyudasya cic-chaktyā kaivalye sthita ātmani You are the original Lord, the controller, beyond material energy. You are situated in your spiritual form, being one with your spiritual energy, and separate from the material energy. SB 1.7.23 Thus everything insignificant is not the object of your anger, but the object of your mercy.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Atha indicate all. The word ca (in another version) indicates buddhi and citta. They all exist in you. Seeing them as separate entities is illusion (bhramaḥ), though matter is eternal and real. “Then what is the fault?” You are without compare (advitīye). Ekam evādvitīyam: the Lord is one without a second. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 6.2.1) Though the moving and non-moving beings are not different from you they are also different. O supreme Lord (bhagavān)! Belonging to you, they are not separate. But you, being bhagavān, are also separate. Or everything is in you, who are without a second. What is the mistake since you are the container and everything is in you? There is no mistake. As your aṁśas, the jīvas, having svarūpas of eternity, knowledge and bliss, are not different from you, having conscious forms. Still, they are like waves in the ocean. There is difference between the ocean and the waves. That is indicated by O bhagavān! The waves are not the ocean and the jīvas are not the Lord.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Shraddha (Faith / Reverence)Aparigraha (Non-possessiveness)Bhakti (Devotion)Saurya (Heroism / Bravery)Kshama (Forgiveness / Patience)Samata (Equanimity / Even-mindedness)Nyaya (Justice / Righteous Judgment)Apaishunam (Non-slander)Vairagya (Detachment / Dispassion)
    Related verses
    BG 14.1 BG 9.11 BG 12.6 BG 17.3 BG 5.18 BG 13.5 BG 18.1 BG 3.10 BG 18.64 BG 10.3 BG 10.4
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    A circle drawn on water seems to enclose a portion of pond as 'separate'; in fact the water on both sides is the same. To imagine earth, water, fire, mind, and senses as independent of Kṛṣṇa is exactly such a drawn circle — the line is bhrama (illusion); the water is Him.

    Stories
    Kapila's enumeration in SB 3

    Lord Kapila, instructing His mother Devahūti, lays out the same list of categories (earth, water, fire, air, ether, mind, intelligence, ahaṅkāra, mahat) and traces each one to Bhagavān. The Sāṅkhya of the Bhāgavatam is theistic — every category resolves into Viṣṇu, not into an impersonal prakṛti.

    Bhūmi's prayer here compresses that long teaching into a single śloka. Gauḍīyas pair these passages in study; the earth-goddess speaks Kapila-tattva.

    Important Words
    • pāda - paṅkajam — lotus feet
      Compound: pāda + paṅkaja. The classical Vaiṣṇava destination — Bhūmi places her grandson at the feet that just removed his father's head. Gauḍīyas read it as the most precise definition of śaraṇāgati: the same hand that struck is the only safe shelter.
    • prapanna - ārti - hara — remover of the distress of those who surrender
      Compound chain: prapanna (surrendered) + ārti (distress) + hara (remover). One of the most important Vaiṣṇava epithets — the Lord is defined by what He does for śaraṇāgatas. Bhūmi's plea is grammatically a name-recitation.
    • hasta - paṅkajam — Your lotus hand
      Compound: hasta (hand) + paṅkaja. Now the hand, after the feet — Bhūmi asks for the same hand that struck Bhauma to rest on the head of Bhauma's son. The image is the chapter's tenderest.
    • kalmaṣa - apaham — remover of all sins
      Compound: kalmaṣa (sin, defilement) + apaham (which takes away). Kalmaṣāpaha — a standard Vaiṣṇava epithet. The Lord's touch is itself prāyaścitta.
    Here is the son of Bhaumāsura. Frightened, he is approaching Your lotus feet, since You remove the distress of all who seek refuge in You. Please protect him. Place Your lotus hand, which dispels all sins, upon his head.
    Purport

    Here the earth-goddess seeks protection for her grandson, who has been frightened by all the terribly violent events that just took place.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Here is the son of Bhaumāsura. Frightened, he is approaching Your lotus feet, since You remove the distress of all who seek refuge in You. Please protect him. Place Your lotus hand, which dispels all sins, upon his head.
    Commentary

    After praising Krsna, Bhumi makes a request.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    “Please say what should be done.” She does not mention her son’s name because he is wicked. “If he is wicked, even if he is your son, he should be killed.” He has approached you. He has surrendered to you. You should destroy the sorrow caused by his father, O destroyer of suffering for the surrendered! Therefore make him fearless. Place your hand on his head. “If I do that, then he will perform evil actions like his father.” That hand destroys all sin. By the power of the touch of your hand he will not commit offenses. The hand is compared to a lotus because it removes all suffering.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Shraddha (Faith / Reverence)Tyaga (Renunciation / Letting Go)Bhakti (Devotion)Yajña (Sacrifice / Spirit of Offering)Daksya (Resourcefulness / Skill)Ishvara Pranidhana (Surrender to God)
    Related verses
    SB 1.2YS 2.45BG 15.7 BG 2.60 BG 18.66 BG 6.35 BG 3.11 BG 12.6 BG 17.3 BG 15.2 BG 5.4 BG 12.5
    Concepts
    Jiva
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    A grandmother places the orphan in the king's lap — and the king, who is also the killer, becomes the protector in the same breath. Bhūmi's request for her grandson is the chapter's secret teaching: Bhagavān's hand can take and the same hand can shelter; surrender knows both grips as one.

    Stories
    Prahlāda asking for his father

    After Nṛsiṁhadeva killed Hiraṇyakaśipu, Prahlāda did not ask for the kingdom — he asked the Lord to deliver his father (SB 7.10.14). The Lord assured him that twenty-one generations of his ancestors were already purified by his birth.

    Bhūmi's prayer here echoes Prahlāda's structurally — a devotee related by blood to a slain demon, requesting shelter for the line. Gauḍīyas pair these: when one drop of bhakti enters a family, even its demoniac members are rescued.

    Important Words
    • bhakti - namrayā — humble with devotion
      Compound: bhakti + namra (bent, humble). Bhakti's posture is namrā — bent, not erect. Gauḍīyas distinguish humility-from-fear (asura-style) from humility-from-love (Bhūmi-style); the verse names the latter.
    • abhayam — fearlessness
      A- (not) + bhaya (fear). Abhaya is one of Bhagavān's classical gifts — bestowed by the abhaya-mudrā of His raised right hand. In the Gītā (BG 16.1) abhaya tops the list of divine qualities; here Kṛṣṇa Himself bestows it.
    • ṛddhi - mat — endowed with opulences
      Compound: ṛddhi (prosperity) + mat (possessing). The palace's wealth — Gauḍīya theology recognizes six bhagas of Bhagavān; even an asura's hoard is finally His property, returning to His hand.
    • bhauma - gṛham — Bhaumāsura's residence
      Compound: Bhauma + gṛha (house). The conqueror enters the conquered's home — and finds inside it the 16,100 women whose rescue is the chapter's second movement.
    Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: Thus entreated by Goddess Bhūmi in words of humble devotion, the Supreme Lord bestowed fearlessness upon her grandson and then entered Bhaumāsura’s palace, which was filled with all manner of riches.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: Thus entreated by Goddess Bhūmi in words of humble devotion, the Supreme Lord bestowed fearlessness upon her grandson and then entered Bhaumāsura's palace, which was filled with all manner of riches.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Worshipped by her words, the Lord was pleased. Instead arcitaḥ sometimes arthitaḥ (pleaded) is seen. kiṣcit karoty urv api yat sva-dattaṁ suhṛt-kṛtaṁ phalgv api bhūri-kārī mayopaṇītaṁ pṛthukaika-muṣṭiṁ pratyagrahīt prīti-yuto mahātmā The Lord considers even his greatest benedictions to be insignificant, while he magnifies even a small service rendered to him by his well-wishing devotee. Thus with pleasure the Supreme Soul accepted a single palmful of the flat rice I brought him. SB 10.81.35 He was pleased by her respect and offerings. The wealth of the city is described in Hari-vaṁśa: na vai rājṣā kubereṇa na śakreṇa yamena ca / ratna-saṁnicayas tādṛg dṛṣṭa-pūrvo na ca śrutaḥ // Never had such quantity of jewels been seen or heard of by any king, Kuvera, Indra or Yama. The demons guarded the jewels. deva-gandharva-ratnāni panna-gānāṁ ca yad vasu / tāni sarvāṇi santīha narakasya niveśane // All the jewels of the devatās and Gandharvas and snakes were in Naraka’s palace.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Worshipped by her words, the Lord was pleased. Instead arcitaḥ sometimes arthitaḥ (pleaded) is seen. By the excellence of her words the Lord was pleased. The words were filled with bhakti. The Lord, full of mercy (bhagavān), bestowed fearlessness to the boy and then entered the palace, to relieve the envy of Satyabhāmā or by getting off Garuḍa. Or he entered with Satyabhāmā, with Garudā, with Bhūmī, with Bhagadatta and with the guardians of the treasury. Because it was the house of the son of Bhūmī it was full of wealth. The wealth of the city is described in Hari-vaṁśa: na vai rājṣā kubereṇa na śakreṇa yamena ca ratna-saṁnicayas tādṛg dṛṣṭa-pūrvo na ca śrutaḥ Never had such quantity of jewels been seen or heard of by any king, Kuvera, Indra or Yama. The demons guarded the jewels. deva-gandharva-ratnāni panna-gānāṁ ca yad vasu tāni sarvāṇi santīha narakasya niveśane All the jewels of the devatās and Gandharvas and snakes were in Naraka’s palace.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    The grandfather grants the grandson's safety with a single word — abhayam — and walks into the palace He has just won. The opulence of the asura's house is suddenly the Lord's; sakala-ṛddhi-mat, every wealth, awaits inside. Devotees note: Kṛṣṇa does not refuse mātarial opulence; He claims it, then redistributes it to His own.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.59.33

    ↗
    तत्र राजन्यकन्यानां षट्‍सहस्राधिकायुतम् । भौमाहृतानां विक्रम्य राजभ्यो दद‍ृशे हरि: ॥ ३३ ॥

    Important Words
    • rājanya - kanyānām — of royal maidens
      Compound chain: rājanya (royal) + kanyā (maiden). Each one a princess by birth — daughters of kings whom Bhauma had defeated. Gauḍīyas note: their dignity-of-birth makes the abduction's wound deeper, and the Lord's restoration more complete.
    • ṣaṭ - sahasra - adhika - ayutam — ten thousand plus six thousand (= sixteen thousand)
      Compound: ṣaṭ (six) + sahasra (thousand) + adhika (more) + ayuta (ten thousand). The famous count — 16,000 here; verse 36 and following round to 16,100 across the wider tradition. Each one becomes Kṛṣṇa's queen.
    • āhṛtānām — taken (by force)
      From ā + √hṛ (to bring, carry off). The same root that names abduction; the women's removal from their fathers' houses is the wound Bhauma inflicted on the kṣatriya dharma.
    • vikramya — by force
      Gerund of vi + √kram (to step boldly, overpower). The same root that named Kṛṣṇa's vikrama in verse 1 — Parīkṣit asked for Kṛṣṇa's vikrama; he received first an account of Bhauma's. Sanskrit poetry weighs roots.
    • dadṛśe — He saw
      Perfect of √dṛś. A single quiet verb at the climax — the Lord enters, and the verse says only 'He saw'. What follows in verses 34-35 explodes from this seeing.
    There Lord Kṛṣṇa saw sixteen thousand royal maidens, whom Bhauma had taken by force from various kings.
    Purport

    Śrīla Śrīdhara Svāmī provides evidence from the sage Parāśara, as quoted in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (5.29.31), to the effect that there were actually 16,100 royal maidens imprisoned in Bhauma’s palace: kanyā-pure sa kanyānāṁ ṣoḍaśātulya-vikramaḥ śatādhikāni dadṛśe sahasrāṇi mahā-mate “Within the maidens’ quarters, O wise one, that Lord of unequaled prowess found 16,100 princesses.” Another relevant verse from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (5.29.9) is as follows: deva-siddhāsurādīnāṁ nṛpānāṁ ca janārdana hṛtvā hi so ’suraḥ kanyā rurodha nija-mandire “The demon [Bhaumāsura] kidnapped the unmarried daughters of demigods, siddhas, asuras and kings, O Janārdana, and imprisoned them in his palace.”

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    There Lord Kṛṣṇa saw sixteen thousand royal maidens, whom Bhauma had taken by force from various kings.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The princesses had been taken by force from various kings.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Hariḥ indicates that he was attractive to the princesses.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    A merchant opens a strongbox to count his gold and finds, instead, sixteen thousand and one hundred living people waiting. Bhauma's hoarded treasure was women — souls — and the moment the Lord steps in, the calculation breaks. They were captives of his lust; they will be queens by His mercy. The exchange is what the whole chapter has been building toward.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.59.34

    ↗
    तं प्रविष्टं स्‍त्रियो वीक्ष्य नरवर्यं विमोहिता: । मनसा वव्रिरेऽभीष्टं पतिं दैवोपसादितम् ॥ ३४ ॥

    Important Words
    • nara - varyam — most excellent of men
      Compound: nara (man) + varya (best, choicest). The princesses see Him first as nara — a man entering the room — and then as nara-varya. The Bhāgavatam never embarrasses the human-face of Bhagavān; that face is the doorway.
    • vimohitāḥ — enchanted
      From vi + √muh (to be bewildered). The same root as moha (delusion) — but here the 'bewilderment' is the auspicious one: charmed-by-the-Lord rather than charmed-by-māyā. Gauḍīyas call this rāga, attraction; it inverts the usual valence of moha.
    • vavrire — they chose
      Perfect of √vṛ (to choose) — the same root that gives svayaṁ-vara, the ceremony of self-choice for a kṣatriya bride. Each princess, in her heart, performs her own svayaṁvara. The Lord is, simultaneously, chosen 16,100 times.
    • daiva - upasāditam — brought by destiny
      Compound: daiva (the divine arrangement) + upasādita (brought near). Gauḍīyas read daiva as Bhagavān's own design — He arranges what He then receives. The grammar of grace.
    The women became enchanted when they saw that most excellent of males enter. In their minds they each accepted Him, who had been brought there by destiny, as their chosen husband.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    The women became enchanted when they saw that most excellent of males enter. In their minds they each accepted Him, who had been brought there by destiny, as their chosen husband.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Seeing him directly (vi—īkṣya) the women were bewildered by the Lord. He was completely desired (abhi—iṣṭam), desired for a long time. He was rare to achieve but they achieved him by previous bhakti (daivopasāditam). They accepted him as their husband. nivasantyo yathā devyaḥ sukhinyaḥ kāma-varjitāḥ // parivavrur mahā-bāhum eka-veṇī-dharāḥ striyaḥ / sarvāḥ kāṣāya-vāsinyaḥ sarvāś ca niyatendriyāḥ // vratopavāsa-tattvajṣāḥ kāṅkṣantyaḥ kṛṣṇa-darśanam / The women living there, devoid of material desire, wearing single braids, accepted Kṛṣṇa in joy. They were all wearing soiled garments. Controlling their senses, practicing vows and fasting, knowing the truth, they desired to see Kṛṣṇa.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Captive birds suddenly see a forest open at the cage door, and each one's heart turns toward the same green — not by agreement but by nature. The princesses, each in her own corner of the prison, each silently choose the same man. Daiva — what the verse calls fate — Gauḍīyas read as the Lord's own arrangement.

    Important Words
    • patiḥ — husband
      From √pā (to protect, to maintain). 'Husband' in Sanskrit is the protector — the same root that names Bhagavān as jagat-pati. Each princess unknowingly addresses Him by His real title.
    • dhātā — providence, the arranger
      From √dhā (to place, ordain). Dhātṛ is one of Brahmā's names — the one who places things in their stations. Each princess prays to dhātā to grant her wish; Gauḍīyas note that the dhātā in this case is Bhagavān Himself, already standing in front of her.
    • kṛṣṇe — in Kṛṣṇa
      Locative of Kṛṣṇa. The princesses 'place their hearts in Kṛṣṇa' — the locative is the bhakta's home case. Gauḍīyas read hṛdayaṁ dadhuḥ as a model of antaḥkaraṇa-arpaṇa, the offering of the inner faculty.
    • bhāvena — with the idea, with emotion
      Instrumental of bhāva — the technical Gauḍīya term for the second stage of bhakti (sādhana-bhakti → bhāva-bhakti → prema-bhakti). The princesses' choice is not a calculation; it is a bhāva.
    With the thought “May providence grant that this man become my husband,” each and every princess absorbed her heart in contemplation of Kṛṣṇa.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    With the thought "May providence grant that this man become my husband," each and every princess absorbed her heart in contemplation of Kṛṣṇa.
    Commentary

    According to the Visnu Purana, there were actually sixteen thousand one hundred women there. Bhauma had stolen the women not only from kings, but from saints and others as well.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    How did they accept him? They each made a vow. The singular is used to show the intensity of the vow of each individual. Thus the word pṛthāk is used. May the Lord (dhātā) who has special powers since he protects the universe make this man Kṛṣṇa, who attracts my heart, my husband.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Shraddha (Faith / Reverence)Bhakti (Devotion)Saurya (Heroism / Bravery)Kshama (Forgiveness / Patience)Samata (Equanimity / Even-mindedness)Nyaya (Justice / Righteous Judgment)Vairagya (Detachment / Dispassion)
    Related verses
    BG 14.1 BG 9.11 BG 12.6 BG 17.3 BG 5.18 BG 9.34 BG 18.1 BG 3.10 BG 18.64 BG 10.3 BG 10.4 BG 6.13 BG 7.8
    Related lectures
    • Padaseva Pr SB 10-59-35 Oriya
      His Grace Padaseva Prabhu· Oriya
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Sixteen thousand small candles, each turning toward the same window where the sun has appeared — and each flame imagining the sun is hers. The miracle of verse 35 is that none of the candles is wrong. Kṛṣṇa belongs to each individually; the others' love does not subtract from one's own.

    Stories
    Gopīs at the rāsa

    In SB 10.33, during the rāsa-līlā, Kṛṣṇa expanded Himself so that each gopī danced with Him alone, each believing herself His exclusive partner. The expansion was not deceit; it was the truth of mādhurya — each devotee's love is met by an undivided Bhagavān.

    The princesses in verse 35 receive a structural echo of that gift. Gauḍīyas distinguish the rāsa-gopīs (svakīya/parakīya mādhurya in Vraja) from the queens of Dvārakā (svakīya mādhurya in the Yadu-pura) — but the same principle obtains: the Lord is somehow undivided in His pluralization.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.59.36

    ↗
    ता: प्राहिणोद्‍द्वारवतीं सुमृष्टविरजोऽम्बरा: । नरयानैर्महाकोशान् रथाश्वान् द्रविणं महत् ॥ ३६ ॥

    Important Words
    • dvāravatīm — to Dvārakā
      Dvāravatī = 'the gated one' — Kṛṣṇa's island capital. Built specifically as a refuge after Mathurā's vulnerability was demonstrated; here it becomes the destination of liberated captives. The city is itself a Vaiṣṇava emblem of shelter.
    • su - mṛṣṭa — well-cleaned
      Compound: su (well) + mṛṣṭa (cleaned, polished). The princesses receive new clean garments — Gauḍīyas note that Kṛṣṇa restores not just freedom but cleanliness; both body and circumstance are made new.
    • virajaḥ - ambarāḥ — spotless garments
      Compound: virajas (dust-free) + ambara (cloth). Vairāgya is etymologically virajas — 'free of rajas (dust/passion)'. The princesses' new clothes are not merely clean; they are vairāgya-clean.
    • nara - yānaiḥ — by human conveyances (palanquins)
      Compound: nara (man) + yāna (vehicle). Not chariots — palanquins, carried by human bearers. Gentle transport, fit for women who have just been rescued.
    The Lord had the princesses arrayed in clean, spotless garments and then sent them in palanquins to Dvārakā, together with great treasures of chariots, horses and other valuables.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    The Lord had the princesses arrayed in clean, spotless garments and then sent them in palanquins to Dvārakā, together with great treasures of chariots, horses and other valuables.
    Commentary

    They travelled to Dvaraka in palanquins (nara yanaih).

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    He sent them to Dvārakā in palanquins. How did they look? They wore clean cloth after bathing in order to become spotless. They were sent along with wealth like jewels, cows and sārika birds. There was excellence because of quantity and value. Those guarding the wealth spoke as follows. imāni maṇi-ratnāni vividhāni vasūni ca / goṣu cāpi bhavet yāvat kāmas tāvaj janārdana / tāvatīḥ prāpayiṣyāmo vṛṣṇy-andhaka-niveśanam // kāma-vyāhāriṇaś caiva pakṣiṇaḥ priya-darśanāḥ // candanāgaru-kāṣṭhāni tathā kālīyakāny api / vasu yat triṣu lokeṣu dharmeṇādhigataṁ tvayā / O Kṛṣṇa! All these jewels and various types of wealth which are here fulfill all desires. There is the same abundance of cows. We will take all of this to Dvārakā. There are pleasantly singing birds, pleasing to see. There are branches of sandalwood and aguru, turmeric and treasures of the three worlds which you have obtained by dharma.

    BG 14.1 BG 9.11 BG 12.6 BG 17.3 BG 5.18 BG 18.1 BG 18.64 BG 10.3 BG 10.4 BG 6.13 BG 7.8 BG 18.37 SB 4.29.1
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    A father retrieving daughters from a hostile house sends them home not in his arms but in palanquins — the dignity of the recovery is in its ceremony. Kṛṣṇa, who has just killed their captor, refuses to handle them roughly; clean garments, palanquins, chariots, horses, treasures. The Bhāgavatam insists on the chivalric reading.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.59.37

    ऐरावतकुलेभांश्च चतुर्दन्तांस्तरस्विन: । पाण्डुरांश्च चतु:षष्टिं प्रेरयामास केशव: ॥ ३७ ॥
    airāvata-kulebhāṁś ca

    Important Words
    • airāvata — Airāvata (Indra's elephant)
      Airāvata — the cosmic white elephant, Indra's mount. Bhauma's elephants are described as descended from Airāvata's family; Kṛṣṇa moves them to Dvārakā. Even Indra's lineage is finally Kṛṣṇa's property.
    • catuḥ - dantān — four-tusked
      Compound: catuḥ (four) + danta (tooth). Four tusks mark elephants of celestial lineage — ordinary elephants have two. A small detail that signals the cosmic register of the chapter's spoils.
    • pāṇḍurān — white
      Pāṇḍura — pale, white. Auspicious color for sacred elephants. The Bhāgavatam loves these chromatic anchors — dark Kṛṣṇa, white elephants, golden chariots.
    • keśavaḥ — Kṛṣṇa (the one with beautiful hair, or 'master of Brahmā and Śiva')
      Keśava — one of Kṛṣṇa's classical names, given two traditional etymologies: 'fine-haired' (keśa) and 'Lord of ka (Brahmā) and īśa (Śiva)'. Either reading exalts the Lord.
    Lord Kṛṣṇa also dispatched sixty-four swift white elephants, descendants of Airāvata, who each sported four tusks.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Lord Kṛṣṇa also dispatched sixty-four swift white elephants, descendants of Airāvata, who each sported four tusks.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The word ca indicates that besides the sixty-four swift elephants almost sixty thousand other elephants were also sent to Dvārakā. Viṣṇu Purāṇa says: catur-daṁṣṭrān gajāṁś cāgryān ṣaṭ-sahasrāṁś ca dṛṣṭavān / kāṁbojānāṁ tathāśvānāṁ niyutāny eka-viṁśatim // tāḥ kanyāstāṁs tathā nāgāṁs tān aśvān dvārakāṁ purīm / prāpayām āsa govindaḥ sadyo naraka-kiṅkaraiḥ // He saw sixty thousand elephants with four tusks and twenty-one million horses from Kāmboja. He sent the princesses, the elephants and horses to Dvārakā along with the servants of Naraka. Hari-vaṁśa gives this description of the guards: bhīma-rūpāś ca mātaṅgāḥ pravāla-vikṛtāṅkuśāḥ // hema-sūtra-mahā-kakṣyāś cāpatomara-śālinaḥ / rucirābhiḥ patākābhir śavalā vividhāḥ kuthāḥ // te ca viṁśati-sāhasrā dvistāvatyaḥ kareṇavaḥ / The elephant keepers were ferocious. They had curved goads made of coral, shirts of gold thread and were equipped with bows and lances. They were made colorful with various attractive flags. They numbered twenty thousand. There were twice as many elephants. Thus other than the four tusked elephants there were many more. The second ca indicates those elephants were also sent. Varuṇa’s umbrella and the jeweled mountain were not sent to Dvārakā but were placed on Garuḍa and went to Svarga. Viṣṇu Purāṇa says ṭadṛśe vāruṇaṁ cchatraṁ tathaiva maṇi-parvatam / āropayām āsa harir garuḍe patageśvare // The Lord placed the umbrella of Varuṇa and the jewel mountain on Garuḍa. tatas tad vāruṇaṁ chatraṁ svayam utkṣipya mādhavaḥ / hiraṇyavarṣaṁ kurvantam āruroha vihaṁgatam // Kṛṣṇa raised up the umbrella of Varuṇa, which produced a shower of gold, and mounted Garuḍa. Later it is said: tasya parvata-rājasya śṛṅgaṁ yat paramārcitam / vimalārkendu-saṁkāśaṁ maṇi-kāṣcana-toraṇam // sapakṣi-gaṇa-mātaṅgaṁ samṛga-vyāla-pādapam / śākhā-mṛga-gaṇair juṣṭaṁ suprastara-śilātalam // nyaṅkubhiś ca varāhaiś ca rurubhiś ca niṣevitam / saprapāta-mahāsānuṁ vicitra-śikhara-drumam // atyadbhutam acintyaṁ ca mṛga-vṛnda-viloḍitam / jīvaṁjīvaka-saṁghaiś ca barhibhiś ca nisevitam // tad apy atibalo viṣṇur dorbhyām utpāṭya bhāsvaram / āropayām āsa tadā garuḍe pakṣiṇāṁ vare // maṇi-parvata-śṛṅgaṁ ca sabhāryaṁ ca janārdanam / uvāha līlayā pakṣī garuḍaḥ patatāṁ varaḥ // The peak of the king of mountains was worthy of supreme worship. It appeared to be a spotless sun and moon, a jeweled gate. It had birds, elephants, deer, snakes, monkey and trees. It was made of broad slabs of rock. On it lived antelope, boars and rurus. It had cliffs, huge plateaus, colorful peaks and trees. Astonishing, inconceivable mountain herds of deer ran about on it. On it lived groups of partridges and peacocks. Mighty Kṛṣṇa lifted the shining mountain with his arms and placed it on Garuḍa, the best of birds. Garuḍa, the best of birds, carried the jeweled mountain peak along with Kṛṣṇa and his wife very easily.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Aparigraha (Non-possessiveness)Bhakti (Devotion)Vairagya (Detachment / Dispassion)Karuna (Kindness / Compassion)

    BG 14.1 BG 9.11 BG 12.6 BG 17.3 BG 5.18 BG 18.1 BG 18.64 BG 10.3 BG 10.4 BG 7.8 BG 18.37 SB 4.29.1 BG 17.20 BG 10.1 BG 11.1
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Sixty-four white four-tusked elephants stream south behind the palanquins like a cloud-train. Kṛṣṇa drains Bhauma's stables into Dvārakā's — the demon hoarded; the Lord redistributes. Bhagavān's economy: nothing accumulates for itself, everything flows toward His own.

    Important Words
    • adityai — to Aditi (Indra's mother)
      Aditi — mother of the devas, wife of Kaśyapa. Her earrings, stolen by Bhauma, are now returned. Gauḍīyas note: Kṛṣṇa's first act after the rescue is to restore a mother's ornaments — bhakti recognizes mothers.
    • pārijātam — the pārijāta tree
      Pārijāta — one of the five wish-fulfilling trees of heaven, produced from the churning of the milk-ocean. Its uprooting becomes the basis of an entire genre of Vaiṣṇava narrative (the Pārijāta-haraṇa).
    • coditaḥ - bhāryayā — urged by His wife
      Compound: codita (impelled) + bhāryā (wife). Bhagavān is moved by Satyabhāmā's wish — a striking image of His responsiveness to His consorts' bhāva, even when (as Gauḍīyas note) the wish provokes war with heaven.
    • nirjitya — defeating
      Gerund of nir + √ji (to conquer). Kṛṣṇa defeats Indra and the demigods to remove the tree — and tomorrow Indra will worship Him again. The asymmetric dance of devotee-rebellion and devotee-return is a Bhāgavata staple.
    The Lord then went to the abode of Indra, the demigods’ king, and gave mother Aditi her earrings; there Indra and his wife worshiped Kṛṣṇa and His beloved consort Satyabhāmā. Then, at Satyabhāmā’s behest the Lord uprooted the heavenly pārijāta tree and put it on the back of Garuḍa. After defeating Indra and all the other demigods, Kṛṣṇa brought the pārijāta to His capital.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    The Lord then went to the abode of Indra, the demigods' king, and gave mother Aditi her earrings; there Indra and his wife worshiped Kṛṣṇa and His beloved consort Satyabhāmā. Then, at Satyabhāmā's behest the Lord uprooted the heavenly pārijāta tree and put it on the back of Garuḍa. After defeating Indra and all the other demigods, Kṛṣṇa brought the pārijāta to His capital.
    Commentary

    Instigated by Satyabhama, Krsna took the parijata tree.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The details of going to Indra’s abode are given in Viṣṇu Purāṇa: garuḍo vāruṇaṁ chatraṁ tathaiva maṇiparvatam / sabhāryaṁ ca hṛṣīkeśaṁ līlayaiva vahan yayau // tataḥ śaṅkham upādhmāsīt svarga-dvāra-gato hariḥ / upatasthus tathā devāḥ sārghya-pātrā janarjanam // sa devair arcitaḥ kṛṣṇo deva-mātur niveśanam / sitābhra-śikharākāraṁ praviśya dadṛśe 'ditim // sa tāṁ praṇamya śakreṇa saha te kuṇḍalottame / dadau naraka-nāśaṁ ca śaśaṁsāsyai janārdanaḥ // Garuḍa very easily carried the umbrella of Varuṇa, the jeweled mountain, Kṛṣṇa and his wife and went to Svarga. The Lord blew his conch when he arrived at the gate of Svarga. The devatās arrived and worshipped Kṛṣṇa with arghya. After being worshipped, Kṛṣṇa entered Aditi’ṣ abode, which resembled a shining peak, and saw her. He offered her respects along with Indra, and presented her with her excellent earrings. He described to Indra the killing of Naraka. Later it is said: adityā tu kṛtānujṣo devarājo janārdanam / yathāvatpūjayāmāsa bahumāna-puraḥsaram // Indra, in gratitude, along with Aditi, worshipped Kṛṣṇa sufficiently, showing great respect. Hari-vaṁśa says: so 'rcito deva-rājena ratnaiś ca pratipūjitaḥ / satyabhāmā ca paulomyā yathāvad abhinanditā // He was worshipped by Indra and presented with jewels. Satyabhāmā was welcomed by Indra’s wife properly. Viṣṇu Purāṇa describes how his wife requested the pārijāta. dadarśa ca sugandhāḍhyaṁ maṣjarī-puṣja-dhāriṇam / śaityāhlādakaraṁ tāmra-bāla-pallava-śobhitam // mathyamāne 'mṛte jātaṁ jāta-rūpamaya-tvacam / pārijātaṁ jagannāthaḥ keśavaḥ keśi-sūdanaḥ // taṁ dṛṣṭvā prāha govindaṁ satyabhāmā dvijottama / kasmān na dvārakām eṣa nīyate kṛṣṇa pādapaḥ // yadi cet tvad vacaḥ satyaṁ satyātyarthaṁ priyeti me / mad geha-niṣkuṭārthāya tad ayaṁ nīyatāṁ taruḥ // // na me jāṁbavatī tādṛg abhīṣṭā na ca rukmiṇī / satye yathā tvam ity uktaṁ tvayā kṛṣṇāsakṛt-priyam // satyaṁ tad yadi govinda nopacāra-kṛtaṁ vacaḥ / tad astu pārijāto 'yaṁ mama geha-vibhūṣaṇam // bibhratī pārijātasya keśa-pakṣeṇa maṣjarīm / sapatnīnām ahaṁ madhye śobheyam iti kāmaye // Kṛṣṇa saw that pārijāta, filled with fragrance, laden with masses of buds, producing coolness and joy, decorated with pink, young shoots and golden bark. It has been produced from the churning of the Milk Ocean. On seeing that tree, Satyabhāmā spoke to Kṛṣṇa, O best of the brāhmaṇas! “O Kṛṣṇa! Why can this tree not be brought to Dvārakā? If your words are true and I am really dear to you, please bring that tree for the garden in my house. O Kṛṣṇa! You have many times said that you do not desire Jāmbavatī or Rukmiṇī as much as you desire me. O Govinda! If you have spoken true and your words are not just a decoration, then the pārijāta should decorate my house. Carrying a bud of the pārijāta in my hair, among all the co-wives I will be glorious. This is what I desire.” Completely conquering (ni-jitya) Indra, he brought the tree to Dvārakā as his reward (upa--anayat) for helping Indra.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Shraddha (Faith / Reverence)Aparigraha (Non-possessiveness)Bhakti (Devotion)Kshama (Forgiveness / Patience)Nyaya (Justice / Righteous Judgment)Apaishunam (Non-slander)Vairagya (Detachment / Dispassion)Karuna (Kindness / Compassion)
    Related verses
    BG 14.1 BG 9.11 BG 12.6 BG 17.3 BG 5.18 BG 18.1 BG 18.65 BG 18.64 BG 10.3 BG 10.4 BG 7.8 BG 18.37
    Related lectures
    • Padaseva Pr SB 10-59-38 Oriya
      His Grace Padaseva Prabhu· Oriya
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Kṛṣṇa returns Aditi's earrings to her face and is worshiped by Indra and Śacī — then, prompted by Satyabhāmā, uproots heaven's prize tree and carries it home on Garuḍa, defeating the very demigods who just worshiped Him. The chapter teaches without saying: heaven's hospitality is conditional; Bhagavān reclaims what is finally His.

    Stories
    Satyabhāmā's competitive love

    Tradition records that Nārada, visiting Kṛṣṇa in Dvārakā, brought a single pārijāta flower and offered it to Rukmiṇī. Satyabhāmā, hearing of it, was wounded; Nārada then suggested that Kṛṣṇa bring the whole tree from heaven for her. Whether one accepts this prelude or not, the Bhāgavatam (here, verse 38-39) records the uprooting as 'urged by His wife'.

    Gauḍīyas read Satyabhāmā's bhāva as īrṣyā-rasa — the loving jealousy that draws Bhagavān into uncommon feats. Indra resists; Kṛṣṇa wins; the tree comes south. Even celestial flora obey conjugal love.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.59.40

    ↗
